ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e3852	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e3867	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I believe that the outcome of the study is extremely problematic and I would also like to explain why. The OECD provides us with too generous a pension system in Austria, and as evidence of this thesis, a maximum pension calculation is used which no longer corresponds to the reality that almost does not occur. Another point: In what form and how the involvement of the second and third pillars has been carried out in other countries, is not understandable. Despite intensive efforts at expert level to understand the OECD-pension model, this has not been possible due to the available transparency. Since Austria has almost total pension expenditure in the first pillar, a comparison that is limited to public pensions is not meaningful if other methods of calculation are used in other countries. In this context, however, the OECD's finding that the capitalised pension funds had to lose 23 percent in the OECD average in 2008, while in Austria we have an average of minus 15 percent. This loss of around a quarter of the value corresponds to a sum that we cannot imagine. In fact, it is 5.4 trillion dollars or 3.9 trillion euros. At the same time, reference is made to the fact that the charging procedure has proved to be a crisis, and that is exactly what my very personal opinion is. The superiority of the charging process in times of economic crisis, the fact that the charging process is much more stable, is far too little outstretched.

With the support of the social partners, with the support of the Minister for Economic Affairs, the Minister for Social Affairs and therefore the Federal Government is on the right track.

This novel is a pilot project whose aim was to catch black sheep through construction site controls, through the building workers' holiday and handling funds. The time between 26th and 26th was very impressive. November 2007 – I have statistics here – until 31. ==References====External links==* Official website In these 568 construction sites, 3 118 construction workers have not been properly reported or the right supplements have not been delivered for vacation and treatment in just over 13 months. ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e12366	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! With the Home Labour Act of 1960, we have a law that has not been modern for a long time, because for years the number of domestic workers has been going back: From 2006 to 2007 alone, the number of companies offering home work has decreased from 179 to 154. And as the statistics show us, in 2009 there is no one interlocutor in Austria, so no one else, who employs domestic workers. There is no longer any need for a special authority with its own bureaucracy in the field of home work. By transferring the previous tasks to the Federal Association Office, a reform of this organisation and the tasks is now to be carried out. However, these measures will also lead to cost reductions, as the changed billing obligations can save around €100 000, and in total, the state, together with the companies concerned, can save more than €200 000 per year or 36 per cent of the previous costs – and all of this without reducing home workers. In the future, the Federal Association Office and the Ministry of Social Affairs will be responsible for home labour. With the innovative home labour law, they ensure that home workers can make their work realistically and effectively. However, there will continue to be written order and delivery conditions, there will continue to be reporting obligations, and there will continue to be a right of observation for the labour inspectorate and health insurance in issue and billing documents, but also for the representatives of interest. In the future, they will continue to support the fact that domestic work is carried out under socially acceptable conditions. Ladies and gentlemen, in general, this is a law that is modern. I just want to say today: We will still pass many laws which, like the labour market or work package, will help many people to get through this crisis without having to purchase the most massive emergency situations. I can only say to you, Minister Hundstorfer, on behalf of my colleagues, whom I represent – and of these 6 000 in short work – that: Thank you for taking these measures for them! Thank you for being able to penetrate the crisis through these measures, and thank you for having a future perspective again!

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e12436	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! With the amendment to the Home Labour Act 1960, we have made it easier for both public and companies to reduce the administrative burden. On the one hand, the paper war is minimized for the companies, and on the other hand, competence is bundled in the Federation. The legal provisions on home work are designed in a contemporary way; for the domestic workers, nothing changes de facto. The people concerned are the domestic workers, intermediate masters, also called piecemasters, who, as we have heard before, de facto no longer exist, as well as middle persons. The structures in the Home Labour Act 1960 are no longer contemporary, and the number of domestic workers, middle-classes and middle-classes has fallen sharply, but the novelle will not lead to material changes for domestic workers. In the sense of organisational and task reform, it is about abolishing and disbanding no longer needed authorities. There is no further need for these authorities. The organization reform provides for the abolition of the Home Workers' Commission and the transfer of the necessary tasks such as the adoption of home labour tariffs and the management of the catastrophe to the Federal Association Office. The deposit of home workers' total contracts is transferred to the Federal Ministry of Labour, Social Affairs and Consumer Protection, since we have already had the collective agreements. On the other hand, we have a job reform, which will bring about the abolition of the fee calculation committee, the abolition of the Home Labour Appeal Commission and the abolition of the Labour Inspectorate's obligation to apply for the fee calculation committee to review the remuneration calculation. They may already recognize the fact that these authorities are no longer modern, and it is very welcome to pool these competences and transfer them to the ministry responsible. For the companies, the following changes will be made: It reduces administrative costs for companies from legal information obligations, e.g. by summarising all reporting obligations for companies with respect to labour inspectorates, and by removing the suspension of working and delivery conditions for companies and simplifying list management for: Enterprises. This generally reduces administrative costs and costs for businesses. This will increase competitiveness in enterprises and, hopefully, will also have a positive impact on employment.

You mentioned here by the Greens that we were in violation of the Convention on Children's Rights, which I decided to reject, Mrs Korun. The Convention on Children's Rights does not prohibit the arrest of minors. Article 37 Child law Convention grants to a minor in the event of his detention certain rights and lays down a general duty of care of the State. This means that a stop can only be made available as a last resort. This regulation was implemented in Sections 77 and 79 of the Foreign Police Act and in the corresponding cases of the BMI. According to the BMI decree, there is no arrest of under-14-year-olds, priority of the relieved drug and retention only Ultima Ratio. In the event of detention, the Child Law Convention provides for a minor to be housed separately from other adults unless another accommodation would be more useful to the child's well-being. That means that the child's well-being is in any case in the foreground. With the Afghan family, the child welfare was also crucial for the children to stay with the father. In accordance with this principle, § 79 para. 2 Foreign Police Act and § 4 of the Rules of Procedure provide for a separate accommodation of minors, but no separation of families is carried out, because it generally serves the child's well-being whenever a child can stay with the parents. In these cases minors of over 14 years of age are held together with their parents or a minor 14-year-old is jointly accommodated with them, but there is no objection to these children.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e17991	High house! It is a pleasant situation – there is also a positive situation in the EU, and I am saying this because it is always “scaring” – if I can report today that by Regulation 544 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 28. In June, our demands were fully taken into account. In fact, this Regulation has been adopted on 1 January 1990. Not only did roaming charges once again lowered or roaming borders were introduced on July 2, but they also – and that is essential – set out measures to eliminate the problem of unexpected high bills. [[President Neugebauer shall take over the chairmanship.]] This will be achieved in future by the fact that mobile operators will always have to provide their roaming customers with individual information on these tariffs if they use a data roaming service in another Member State. In addition, mobile operators are in order to avoid so-called billing shocks from 1. March 2010 also continued to offer us not only one but even several ceilings and to report when we come to the close of these financial ceilings. This is essential to me because I can then decide as a customer whether I want to continue to receive these services or not. This means that, in future, this regulation will require the transfer of basic personalised tariff information to my computer at the time of use on my mobile phone in the form of text messages or e-mail – and this also in an easy-to-understand form so that we know what is happening to us. That is why I did not understand, because these requests go much more, much more than what we have dealt with in the Consumer Protection Committee, dear colleague Mr. Zanger and my colleague, dear, that you have not withdrawn your requests. One should also take note of this positively.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e19549	Next speaker: Mrs Dr. Karl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e19571	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! If my previous speaker complains that we have too few minority rights in the Austrian Parliament, I must already point out that a Europe-wide comparison has been clear and clear that we, in Europe, have the most minority-friendly parliament at all! [[Call the FPÖ:Thank you, Mrs Deputies!]] Please note! That's a fact. You can also take note of these documents, which is no problem at all. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Dr. Moser:... the German Parliament!]] [[Further calls.]] However, I would now like to comment on the real subject of this request. In the context of the implementation of the AUA Privatisation Order, it has been repeatedly claimed that the procedure has been unlawfully carried out. In order to review these allegations, ÖIAG has commissioned two legal opinions. The experts Professor Novotny and Professor Eilmansberger were at the disposal of information in the Court of Auditors' Subcommittee and informed us in detail about the European law requirements for privatisation processes and their importance for AUA privatisation. Let me briefly consider the results of the two legal scientists. The need for bidders to have sufficient time and information to be able to carry out an appropriate assessment of the assets to which their offer relates was best met in the current privatisation procedure. The procedure chosen by the ÖIAG to provide the tenderers with access to sensitive information only gradually and with the intensity of participation in the procedure was considered admissible. It is also quite common for private business sales to gradually structure access to information in this or similar way. The state restructuring aid required by Lufthansa for the AUA in the form of a debt release amounting to €500 million, which has already been addressed, is also proved unproblematic. The offer of a negative purchase price by Lufthansa instead of a positive purchase price is therefore a process-compliant condition, according to the experts. In part, it has also caused excitement that the privatisation order empowers ÖIAG to privatise the AUA while maintaining an Austrian core shareholder structure of 25 percent plus a share. In order to fulfil this privatisation mandate, a model of the foundation was chosen. Foundation structures for safeguarding national interests have also been taken into account in the privatisation of the KLM and Swiss. Professor Novotny pointed out that such a concept corresponds to the specifications of the privatisation contract and that the bodies of the ÖIAG are legally acting. Here, therefore, we have to do with a well thought-out, meaningful foundation. Given the recent experience with foundations, it is not often enough to stress that Foundation is not the same Foundation. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e21856	Ladies and gentlemen, It was previously complained by my previous speaker that the last meeting of the Scientific Committee had taken place during the sitting-free time. – That is true, but the reason for this is that it was not possible to find an appointment in the two committee weeks. But now on the real topic: My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, in the run-up to the UG-Novelle there were many fears which have now proved to be unfounded. The fearsome restriction of free access to higher education is no more than the reduction of the Senate in the election of the rectors, a budget reserve for universities in financial difficulties, or the dreaded politicization of universities. – In all these points, warning can be given. The planned entry phase is intended to give students an overview of the essential contents and the course of their studies and should not be used as a quantitative restriction on access. While qualitative access limits can be set for Master and PhD studies, there must be a guaranteed Master's connection for each Bachelor's degree at a university, which has no other access conditions than the graduation of Bachelor's degree. The choice of the rector or rector will be supported by a finding commission in the future, without restricting the central function of the Senate. There will continue to be no rector and no rector against the will of the Senate. With regard to the retention of a budget reserve, it should be noted that the Minister of Science cannot, as originally planned, retain up to 5 per cent, but only 2 per cent of the budget. This money is allocated by annual design agreements with universities, for example, for the initiation of new scientific developments or for the improvement of care relationships. The concerns about the politicalisation of the universities were also taken seriously, so it was not possible to abolish the so-called 'blocking clause' for politicians. In addition, there are a number of measures which bring significant improvements for universities as a whole, but also specifically for teachers and students. These include, for example, the improvement of the conditions of study, the use of accommodating skills, and the improvement of the quality of life. promotion, safeguarding the development of the study site and safeguarding the domestic interests of German Numerus-clausus refugees, the upgrading of the academic medium-sized building and the opening of new career opportunities for middle-building staff, the more efficient design of habilitation and appeal procedures, and a clearer distribution of competence between university councils, Senate and rectorate. In particular, however, it is important to strengthen the participation rights of the Works Councils in the University Council. In the future, the chairperson of the two works councils will be able to exercise their constitutional powers in the University Council. They may apply for information, advice and consent rights in matters falling within the competence of the University Council. In order to discuss and decide on such applications, they may request their own agenda items and shall also be entitled to vote on those agenda items when deciding on them. In order to ensure their rights of information, a copy of the minutes of the meeting of the University Council must be transmitted to them immediately. ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e22154	Ladies and gentlemen! Federal Minister! From a social-democratic point of view, the negotiations on the innovation of the 2002 UG were a very delicate challenge, on the one hand, because it was a law that we did not agree with at the time, because it did not correspond to our ideas of democratic structures at universities, and on the other hand, because the starting point for these negotiations was a draft assessment, which did not lead to a series of enthusiasm storms in the universities, but was very critical. From today's point of view, I can say that the novelle, as it stands, is very clearly different from the assessment draft, and the difference between the draft assessment and the novelle is the most important part of the social democratic manuscript in this novel. The fact that Mr Karl is standing here today and, as a success, is a point of point, which has not come into this novel, although originally planned, I can quite count as an unexpected success of our conviction during these negotiations. The concerns expressed on this draft assessment have, in particular, affected a possible limitation of university autonomy, the fear of greater political intervention and the fear that the university's budgetary margin would be too limited. A point that was particularly important to us beyond these points, which was not or hardly addressed in the examination procedure, was the concern of improving the study conditions. On all these points, we were able to move very clearly in the negotiations. Senate tag: Yes, the Senate is the last directly elected institution in the universities in which all the groups of universities are represented – all other places of discussion and democratic participation in the universities have been abolished. Originally, it was planned, particularly in a very important area to the Senate, namely the choice of rectors, to shift the skills away from the Senate. Here, we have agreed – and this is the case today – on a model which is a very fair one, which is also recognized by the universities accordingly, through which it will still not be possible to order a rector or a rector against the will of the Senate. As far as the budgetary margin is concerned, the design agreements have also changed: We will not be able to hold back budgetary resources as planned, and we have now made sure, in the amendment that we have still decided on the Committee, that these funds, of course, have to go back to universities to their full extent. As far as the improvement of the study conditions is concerned, in the future there will be points to be negotiated with universities which have not yet been negotiated and which are very important from the point of view of students. Measures will have to be taken to reduce the number of drop-outs/retirements, to improve the care relationship, to increase job-accompanied offers, to provide support for students with children. Another important point to improve students is that there is an additional reason for exemption for tuition fees: In the future, those receiving study aid will no longer have to pay tuition fees. This is also a way of exempting some of those who now have to pay study fees for multiple studies. We have managed to define a very fair and transparent study entry phase, which does not have a Knock-out test, which will not be a Knock-out phase and will ensure that students will continue to have several opportunities when they start their studies in the future. As far as access to Master's degree is concerned, the government programme has been set up in accordance with the possibility of qualitative conditions of access, but subject to the condition that students are offered at the respective university. continuing professionally at least one access to a master's degree must be free without further requirements. In this respect, we have also decided on a committee in the Scientific Committee, which makes it clear. I say this in full consciousness: This is a regulation that we must observe as such and its impact over the next few years, so we have also limited the time limit for this regulation. And we will observe very closely whether this regulation at the universities is not misused to create two classes of master's studies, because in this sense this regulation is not intended. One important point was also the expansion of women's promotion at universities – the 40 percent quota has already been addressed –: We do not only anchor the 40 percent quota, we also want a report by the universities if this quota is not fulfilled, what measures are to be envisaged and what timetable this quota should be reached on. The aim is to increase the proportion of women in executive functions and, above all, to promote young researchers – with small ‘i’. Improvements will be made for medium-sized construction, for the upgrading and strengthening of the members of the medium-sized building – a group that is highly qualified and has a significant part of the work at universities. There will be better career opportunities for middle construction members and better participation opportunities. The Senate will be re-composed in the future: The professors will no longer have more than 50 percent of the votes, and the middle construction will have more seats here. More participation is also the key word for the expansion of the participation rights of works councils in the University Council: The works councils will have a voting right in the University Council in future. Ladies and gentlemen! In total, there is a novel that, from my point of view, represents a good condition, a good law, a good innovation for sustainable universities in our country.

In the field of tertiary education in particular, Austria has shown in the international comparison in recent years that there is a lot of need for recovery. Unfortunately, the law adopted in 2002 has not really improved this situation. The more important it was now to carry out an evaluation and quickly decide on a novel of this law. From a social democratic point of view, it is especially the improvements for students, young researchers and, of course, for women at universities that are particularly prominent. This will at least improve the 2002 law. Of course, there are still a number of points to be negotiated in the framework of the service agreements between the ministries and universities: the measures that need to be put in place to promote the compatibility of childcare and a scientific career, more and better offers for working students – of which I can sing a painful song myself – and a sufficient range of courses for students. There will also be some change in the composition of the top democratic institutions. There will now no longer be an absolute majority for the Curia of ordinary professors. In the future, representatives of the Works Council will also be invited to attend the meetings of the University Council, and they will also receive a voting right. New in all bodies is also the duty rate of a 40 percent female share – terrifying enough that such a quota in science is still necessary in 2009. All in all, more evaluation reports on the University Law of 2002 are needed, but this innovation was done in the right direction. I would also like to thank our chief negotiator, Andrea Kuntzl, for her strong, active and excellent commitment in recent months. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e23451	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The stabilisation of the Balkans is a classic concern of Austrian and European foreign policy. We have been working on this subject for decades. One of the most important instruments in stabilisation, in the creation of more security, of more prosperity in the Balkan countries, is the so-called We have today reached an agreement on relations between the European Union and Bosnia and Herzegovina, called SAP, the stabilisation and association process. The purpose of this agreement is to consolidate, strengthen and strengthen relations. It is a customized individual agreement for this country. It offers a wide range of measures to support, in particular in the area of the rule of law, those to take action in the area of security, such as anti-corruption measures or the fight against organised crime, money laundering, drug trafficking and illegal migration. There are also a number of provisions relating to economic cooperation, which is also one of the objectives in the stabilisation of the region. This means that those Austrian companies with trade relations or investment relations with Bosnia and Herzegovina will also have improved legal framework in the future. It is this, my Ladies and gentlemen – and this is important – the sixth agreement of this kind. With the exception of Kosovo, for which a separate procedure has been envisaged, we have thus captured all the Balkan countries, all neighbouring countries to the southeast, so to speak, with this process. It is an important step that we are taking in this agreement, and I am convinced that the consensus of this House on our European and Austrian Balkan policy will continue here today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] It is important on this issue that the Balkan countries determine the pace by which they take this European path. In each of these agreements, a strict conditionality is agreed, i.e. progress cannot be made without the fulfilment of certain conditions and certain criteria. This is an encouragement and an incentive, but also always a topic of discussions, for example, when we consider an Austrian initiative that will continue to concern us in the near future, namely the topic of visa facilitation. In this regard, we have proposed to Austrians that there should be appropriate roadmaps. The implementation of this project is now entering a new phase of implementation, and so far not all countries have been at the same level of implementation or at the same level and cannot yet be treated in the same way. However, the course is clear and correct: It goes to Europe, and here it is – this is an important foreign policy concern, ladies and gentlemen! – do not give art breaks with respect for artificial breaks. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It must not come to rest by linking things that have nothing to do with each other. “Partners, not Protectorate”, that is the title under which these so-called SAA agreements could sail with the Balkan countries, because this is where help is given to help self-help in the best tradition of European foreign policy. ‘Partners, not Protectorate’ means a demanding programme, especially for Bosnia-Herzegovina. We have experienced in recent weeks the exchange of views on the controversial decisions of the Parliament of the Republic of Srpska. I am convinced that the Austrian top diplomat Valentin Inzko has done quite well to make these controversial decisions, which are not in accordance with Dayton and with the basics of the Bosnian Constitution stood to end with the support of the international community. We too wish for the day when the "Bonn Powers", that is, the large majority of the High Representative's rights, will no longer be necessary, and we are actively working on favouring a situation in which this will no longer be necessary. However, I would also expressly like to congratulate, on your behalf, Ambassador Inzko on his difficult work and thank him for this step he has fully justified. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-09-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00031_d8e23559	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Stabilisation and Association Agreement between the European Union, on the one hand, and Bosnia and Herzegovina, on the other, is of vital importance. Similar agreements have already been concluded with Albania, Croatia, the former Yugoslav Republic of Macedonia, Montenegro and Serbia and have provided valuable services there. The contents of this agreement have already been explained in detail by my predecessor, Mrs Plassnik. It is about promoting the establishment of a democratic rule of law, and it is about respect for human rights and, of course, minority rights. Above all, it is also about building functioning market economic structures, not least by creating a free trade area. There are also very promising structures for economic relations with Austria, and there are quite random opportunities. So this is a win-win situation for both sides, but of course it is not just about economic goals, it is mainly about peacekeeping on our continent. In the suffering-tested Balkans, stability and security are still a delicate plant, which needs intensive care so that it can grow into a robust tree, which is particularly resistant to the storms of nationalism. How strong these storms are, Valentin Inzko must still know, and we can only congratulate our fellow countryman on how he manages this difficult situation as a High Representative and consistently opposes nationalist cross-scores. With this agreement, we are taking a further step in this direction and thus in the direction of sustainable peace throughout Europe.

This is also the Austrian bonus, which is to be borne here, namely the high willingness to perform, the high willingness to education and training. I would like to address the question of why the budgetary situation has changed. We have long pressed for the tax reform that came this year, and we are very proud that implementation has succeeded. We had already changed it in 2008 It is a very important measure for many, for workers, for SMEs, for small and medium-sized enterprises. The tax reform is also the right answer to this economic situation and also the right answer to the question of fiscal justice, which unfortunately occurred in the years before. In one sentence I can mention something that is not reflected in the figures but in the political climate. It was also the year 2006/2007, in which it came to the “social partnership-new”. Once the social partners have been faced with rapid decisions, it is now customary to discuss the projects with the social partners. The new budgetary law has already been mentioned. Here, too, the federal states are required. New budgetary law brings more transparency, and this is precisely what is needed in many federal states. I would like to see additional transparency in the Land budget of Upper Austria. Thank you.
